If the challenged arbitrator withdraws, this shall be without prejudice to the right of the challenging party to pursue its challenge. In both cases, the arbitral proceedings shall be suspended until a replacement arbitrator has been appointed. The suspension of the arbitral proceedings shall be without prejudice to the right of the arbitral tribunal to continue the proceedings.

**5.** If any party does not agree to the challenge, the challenging party may refer the matter to the appointing authority for decision. The decision of the appointing authority on the challenge shall be final.

### Article 14 – Replacement of an Arbitrator

**1.** An arbitrator shall be replaced if the arbitrator withdraws, or the parties agree that the arbitrator's mandate shall be terminated, or the arbitrator's mandate is otherwise terminated.

**2.** If an arbitrator is replaced, the proceedings shall resume at the stage they had reached when the vacancy occurred, unless the arbitral tribunal decides otherwise.

**3.** The replacement arbitrator shall be appointed pursuant to the procedure set out in articles 8 to 10, and the proceedings shall resume from the stage they had reached when the vacancy occurred or when the arbitral tribunal decided to resume the proceedings.

**4.** If a sole arbitrator is replaced, the proceedings shall resume at the stage they had reached when the vacancy occurred, unless the parties agree otherwise.

### Article 15 – Repetition of Hearings in the Event of the Replacement of an Arbitrator

**1.** If an arbitrator is replaced, the new arbitrator shall decide whether any prior hearings shall be repeated. If the new arbitrator decides that prior hearings shall be repeated, the repeat hearings shall take place within a period of time to be fixed by the new arbitrator.

**2.** If the new arbitrator decides that prior hearings shall not be repeated, the new arbitrator shall determine, after consultation with the parties, what further proceedings, if any, are necessary.

### Article 16 – Exclusion of Liability

**1.** Neither the Secretary-General nor any member of the International Bureau nor any arbitrator shall be liable to any person for any act or omission in connection with any arbitration under these Rules, unless such limitation of liability is excluded by applicable law.

**2.** The preceding paragraph shall be without prejudice to any liability of the parties under applicable law.

### SECTION III. ARBITRAL PROCEEDINGS

### Article 17 – General Provisions

**1.** Subject to these Rules, the arbitral tribunal may conduct the arbitration in such manner as it considers appropriate, provided that the parties are treated with equality and that at an appropriate stage of the proceedings each party is given a reasonable opportunity to present its case.

**2.** As soon as practicable after its constitution, the arbitral tribunal shall inform the parties of the general procedure it intends to follow, including whether it intends to apply any techniques for the efficient management or resolution of disputes, such as case management conferencing, procedural timetables, or document production. The arbitral tribunal shall consult with the parties with respect to such general procedure and shall thereafter be guided by any agreement between the parties on such matters.

**3.** The arbitral tribunal shall, within a reasonable time after its constitution, establish the terms of reference or, in the absence of such terms of reference, define its tasks and duties in relation to the arbitration. The terms of reference shall, or such definition shall, include a list of the issues to be decided and shall be communicated to the parties and the International Bureau.

### Article 18 – Place of Arbitration

**1.** The parties may agree on the place of arbitration. Failing such agreement, the place of arbitration shall be determined by the arbitral tribunal having regard to all relevant circumstances.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal may, unless otherwise agreed by the parties, meet at any place it considers appropriate for hearings, for deliberations, or for inspections of goods, other property or documents. The arbitral tribunal shall give the parties sufficient notice of such meetings.

### Article 19 – Language

**1.** Subject to any agreement by the parties, the arbitral tribunal shall determine the language or languages to be used in the proceedings. This determination shall apply to the statement of claim and defence, to any hearings and, if the arbitral tribunal considers it appropriate, to any other submissions in the proceedings.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal may order that any documents delivered in another language shall be accompanied by a translation into the language or languages so determined.

### Article 20 – Statement of Claim

**1.** Within a period of time to be determined by the arbitral tribunal, the claimant shall submit to the tribunal and the other parties a statement of claim containing:
(a) The names and addresses of the parties;
(b) A description of the circumstances that give rise to the dispute;
(c) A description of the claims and the legal grounds, or a statement of the facts and law on which each claim is based; and
(d) The relief or remedy sought.

**2.** The claimant shall annex to the statement of claim all documents it relies on, or shall submit a list of such documents.

### Article 21 – Statement of Defence

**1.** Within a period of time to be determined by the arbitral tribunal, the respondent shall submit to the tribunal and the other parties a statement of defence containing:
(a) The name and address of the respondent;
(b) A statement responding to each of the claimant's claims, facts and legal grounds, or a statement of the facts and law on which each response is based;
(c) Any counterclaim or set-off, together with the documents and information on which the respondent relies in support thereof; and
(d) The relief or remedy sought in relation to any counterclaim.

**2.** The respondent shall annex to the statement of defence all documents on which it relies, or shall submit a list of such documents.

### Article 22 – Amendments to the Claim or Defence

**1.** During the course of the arbitral proceedings, a party may amend or supplement its claims or defence, including any counterclaim or set-off, unless the arbitral tribunal considers it inappropriate to allow such amendment, having regard to the delay in making it or prejudice to the other party or any other relevant circumstance. A counterclaim may not be made if the arbitral tribunal considers it outside the scope of the arbitration agreement.

**2.** No party shall be bound by any amendment or supplement it makes to its claims or defence unless it is accepted by the other parties or approved by the arbitral tribunal.

### Article 23 – Pleas as to the Jurisdiction of the Arbitral Tribunal

**1.** The arbitral tribunal shall have the power to rule on its own jurisdiction, including any objection to the existence or validity of the arbitration agreement. For that purpose, an arbitration clause that forms part of a contract shall be treated as an agreement independent of the other terms of the contract. A decision by the arbitral tribunal that the contract is null and void shall not entail ipso jure the invalidity of the arbitration clause.

**2.** A plea that the arbitral tribunal does not have jurisdiction shall be raised no later than in the statement of defence or, with respect to a counterclaim or set-off, in the reply to the counterclaim. A party shall not be precluded from raising such a plea by the fact that it has appointed or participated in the appointment of an arbitrator. A plea that the arbitral tribunal is exceeding the scope of its authority shall be raised promptly after the arbitral tribunal has indicated its intention to decide on a matter not covered by the arbitration agreement or, as the case may be, after the arbitral tribunal proceeds on that matter.

**3.** The arbitral tribunal may, in the event of a plea as referred to in paragraph 2, either on the basis of the documents produced by the parties or after hearing the parties, rule on the plea as a preliminary question or join such plea to the merits of the case. The arbitral tribunal may continue the arbitral proceedings and make an award notwithstanding any pending challenge to its jurisdiction.

### Article 24 – Further Written Statements

**1.** The arbitral tribunal shall decide which further written statements, in addition to the statement of claim and statement of defence, shall be required from the parties or may be presented by them, and shall fix the periods of time for submitting such statements.

**2.** The periods of time fixed for the submission of the written statements should not exceed 45 days. However, the arbitral tribunal may extend such time limits if it considers such extension justified.

### Article 25 – Periods of Time

**1.** The periods of time fixed by the arbitral tribunal for the submission of written statements, including the statement of claim and statement of defence, may be extended by agreement of the parties or by the arbitral tribunal if it considers such extension justified.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal may direct that the reply to any counterclaim and the answer to any reply shall be submitted within a fixed period of time.

### Article 26 – Interim Measures

**1.** At the request of a party, the arbitral tribunal may grant interim measures in accordance with the applicable law.

**2.** The interim measures shall be formulated in a decision. The arbitral tribunal shall be entitled to order the parties to provide appropriate security in connection with a measure it has ordered.

**3.** A request for interim measures addressed by a party to any judicial authority shall not be deemed incompatible with the agreement to arbitrate, or as a waiver of that agreement. Such request shall not，哪怕是 deemed to be a waiver of the right of the arbitral tribunal to order appropriate interim measures.

**4.** The arbitral tribunal may order the preservation, storage, sale or other disposal of any property or assets that are the subject matter of the dispute.

### Article 27 – Evidence

**1.** Each party shall have the burden of establishing the facts in support of its claim or defence.

**2.** Witnesses, including expert witnesses, who are presented by a party to testify to the arbitral tribunal shall be heard by the tribunal. The tribunal may also hear other witnesses or expert witnesses not presented by either party with the agreement of the parties.

**3.** The parties may agree, or the arbitral tribunal may order, that witnesses, including expert witnesses, are to be examined by testimony, cross-examination or other methods.

**4.** The arbitral tribunal may direct that the testimony of witnesses, including expert witnesses, be presented in written form, either as signed statements or sworn affidavits.

**5.** Subject to any limitations agreed by the parties or ordered by the arbitral tribunal, all documents, records, and other materials supplied by one party to the other shall be made available to the other party.

**6.** The arbitral tribunal shall determine the admissibility, relevance, materiality and weight of the evidence offered.

### Article 28 – Hearings

**1.** In the event that an oral hearing is to be held, the arbitral tribunal shall give the parties adequate advance notice of the date, time and place of any such hearing.

**2.** Witnesses and experts may be heard at a hearing and examined, cross-examined or re-examined, as the case may be, under the supervision of the arbitral tribunal.

**3.** Hearings shall be held in private unless the parties agree otherwise.

**4.** The arbitral tribunal shall have the power to require the production of documents, exhibits or other evidence by the parties within such time as the arbitral tribunal shall determine.

### Article 29 – Experts Appointed by the Arbitral Tribunal

**1.** After consultation with the parties, the arbitral tribunal may appoint one or more independent experts to report to it on specific issues designated by the arbitral tribunal.

**2.** The expert shall, upon receipt of the information and documents requested by the arbitral tribunal, include in his or her report a statement of his or her findings and conclusions on the issues referred to him or her, together with a statement of the expertise and methodology used.

**3.** Copies of the expert's report shall be transmitted to the parties, which shall have an opportunity to express their opinion on the report.

**4.** Upon receipt of the expert's report, the arbitral tribunal shall, upon the request of a party, provide such party with an opportunity to question the expert at a hearing. In such hearing, the parties may present expert witnesses to testify on points of substance.

### Article 30 – Default

**1.** If the claimant fails to submit its statement of claim in accordance with article 20, the arbitral tribunal shall terminate the proceedings.

**2.** If the respondent fails to submit its statement of defence in accordance with article 21, the arbitral tribunal shall order the proceedings to continue.

**3.** If a party fails to appear at a hearing or to produce documentary evidence, the arbitral tribunal may continue the proceedings and make the award on the evidence before it.

### Article 31 – Closure of Proceedings

**1.** When satisfied that the parties have had a reasonable opportunity to present their cases, the arbitral tribunal shall declare the proceedings closed.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal may, on its own initiative or upon the application of a party, reopen the proceedings at any time before the award is made if it considers it necessary to do so.

### Article 32 – Waiver of Right to Object

**1.** A party who knows that any provision of these Rules or any requirement under the arbitration agreement has not been complied with, and who fails to state promptly its objection to such non-compliance, shall be deemed to have waived its right to object.

### SECTION IV. THE AWARD

### Article 33 – Decisions

**1.** When there is more than one arbitrator, any decision of the arbitral tribunal shall be made by a majority of the arbitrators. If any arbitrator refuses to participate in a decision for any reason, the majority may proceed and decide the matter, provided that the refusing arbitrator has been given reasonable notice and opportunity to participate.

**2.** In the case of questions of procedure, when there is no majority or when the arbitration is to be conducted by a sole arbitrator, the presiding arbitrator may decide on such questions of procedure, if authorized to do so by the arbitral tribunal or, failing such authorization, after consultation with the other arbitrators.

### Article 34 – Form and Effect of the Award

**1.** The award shall be made in writing and shall state the reasons on which it is based, unless the parties have agreed that no reasons are to be given.

**2.** The award shall state the date and place of the arbitration in accordance with article 18.

**3.** The award shall be signed by the arbitrators and shall contain a statement as to the quorum.

**4.** If any arbitrator refuses to sign the award, the signature of the majority shall be sufficient and shall state the reason for the absence of the other signatures.

**5.** The award shall be delivered to each party by sending copies thereof by registered post or by courier, or by any other means that provides a record of delivery or attempted delivery. Copies shall also be transmitted to the parties by the International Bureau.

**6.** By agreeing to arbitration under these Rules, the parties undertake to carry out any award without delay.

**7.** The award shall be final and binding on the parties from the date it is made.

### Article 35 – Applicable Law, Amiable Compositeur

**1.** The arbitral tribunal shall apply the rules of law designated by the parties as applicable to the substance of the dispute. Failing such designation by the parties, the arbitral tribunal shall apply such rules of law as it determines to be appropriate.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal shall decide as amiables compositeurs only if the parties have expressly authorized the arbitral tribunal to do so.

**3.** The arbitral tribunal shall not decide as ex aequo et bono unless the parties have expressly authorized the arbitral tribunal to do so.

**4.** In all cases, the arbitral tribunal shall decide in accordance with the terms of the contract, if any, and shall take into account any usage of trade applicable to the transaction.

### Article 36 – Settlement or Other Grounds for Termination

**1.** If the parties reach a settlement before an award is made, the arbitral tribunal shall either issue an order for the termination of the arbitral proceedings or, if requested by the parties and accepted by the tribunal, record the settlement in the form of a consent award.

**2.** If it becomes unnecessary or impossible for the arbitral tribunal to continue the arbitral proceedings for any reason, the arbitral tribunal shall inform the parties of its intention to issue an order for the termination of the proceedings. The arbitral tribunal shall have the power to issue such an order unless a party raises justifiable grounds for objection.

**3.** The order for termination or the consent award shall be issued by the arbitral tribunal and shall state the circumstances of the termination.

**4.** A consent award shall be made in accordance with the provisions of article 34.

### Article 37 – Interpretation of the Award

**1.** Within 30 days after receipt of the award, any party may, with notice to the other party, request that the arbitral tribunal give an interpretation of the award.

**2.** The interpretation shall be given in writing within 45 days after receipt of the request. The interpretation shall form part of the award.

### Article 38 – Correction of the Award

**1.** Within 30 days after receipt of the award, any party may, with notice to the other party, request that the arbitral tribunal correct any clerical, typographical or similar errors in the award.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal may correct the award on its own initiative within 30 days after the date of the award.

**3.** Such corrections shall be in writing and shall form part of the award. The provisions of article 34, paragraphs 4 to 6, shall apply.

### Article 39 – Additional Award

**1.** Within 30 days after receipt of the award, any party may, with notice to the other party, request that the arbitral tribunal make an additional award as to claims or counterclaims presented in the proceedings but not decided in the award.

**2.** If the arbitral tribunal considers the request for an additional award to be justified, it shall complete its work on the additional award within 60 days after receipt of the request.

**3.** The additional award shall be made in accordance with the provisions of article 34.

### Article 40 – Definition of Costs

**1.** The arbitral tribunal shall fix the costs of arbitration in the award.

**2.** The term "costs" includes only:
(a) The fees of the arbitral tribunal, which shall be fixed individually by the appointing authority or directly by the parties, as the case may be;
(b) The reasonable travel and other expenses incurred by the arbitrators;
(c) The reasonable costs for expert advice and for other assistance required by the arbitral tribunal;
(d) The reasonable travel and other expenses of witnesses, to the extent approved by the arbitral tribunal;
(e) The costs for legal representation and assistance of the successful party, if such costs were claimed during the arbitration and the arbitral tribunal determines them to be reasonable;
(f) Any fees and expenses of the appointing authority.

### Article 41 – Fees and Expenses of Arbitrators

**1.** Each arbitrator shall be entitled to request the payment of fees and expenses in accordance with a fee schedule fixed by the appointing authority, if any, or otherwise in accordance with such fee schedule as the parties may agree, or, failing such agreement, as the arbitral tribunal shall determine.

**2.** If the appointing authority fixes the arbitrators' fees and expenses, it shall apply the fee schedule in effect on the date of the commencement of the arbitration.

### Article 42 – Allocation of Costs

**1.** The costs of arbitration shall in principle be borne by the unsuccessful party or parties. However, the arbitral tribunal may apportion such costs between the parties if it determines that apportionment is reasonable, taking into account the circumstances of the case.

**2.** The arbitral tribunal shall, in its discretion, apportion the costs of arbitration if it determines that such apportionment is reasonable. In exercising such discretion, the arbitral tribunal may take into account such factors as it considers relevant, including the manner in which the parties conducted the arbitration.

**3.** With respect to the costs of legal representation and assistance referred to in article 40, paragraph 2(e), the arbitral tribunal, in exercising its discretion under paragraphs 1 and 2, may take into account the outcome of the arbitration and the conduct of the parties.

### Article 43 – Deposit of Costs

**1.** The arbitral tribunal may require the parties to deposit an equal amount in advance to cover the costs referred to in article 40, paragraphs 2(a) to (d).

**2.** If the matter is not yet at a stage where such a deposit may be required, the arbitral tribunal shall request the deposit when appropriate.

**3.** The arbitral tribunal may require the parties to deposit additional amounts as the proceedings progress.

**4.** If a deposit is not paid within 15 days after receipt of the request, the arbitral tribunal shall so inform the parties in order that one or another of them may make the required payment.

**5.** If neither party makes the required payment, the arbitral tribunal may order the suspension or termination of the arbitration.

**Fresa Li:**

PCA Arbitration Rules are an optimized version based on the UNCITRAL Arbitration Rules, making them the most historically significant and broadly representative institutional framework in the international arbitration system, and the authoritative framework for inter-State and investor-State disputes. The 2012 edition and the 2024 Optional Protocol together form the foundational framework for State-to-State dispute resolution. At the operational level, the PCA's administrative flexibility is notable—its ability to serve as appointing authority under both ad hoc and institutional frameworks, combined with the Peace Palace's facilities for hearings involving multiple States, adds legitimacy to sensitive proceedings. Practitioners in investor-State disputes should note that the PCA's financial regulations impose cost structures distinct from commercial institutions, which must be factored into the claims strategy.

---


**Dr. Venessa:**

The PCA is the only institution among the ten handling state-to-state disputes — its irreplaceable institutional value and its heaviest Agent-era burden. The 2024 Optional Protocols added emergency interim measures and expedited procedures built on UNCITRAL Rules. But AI involvement in state disputes differs fundamentally from commercial arbitration: autonomous weapons system accountability algorithmic trade sanction legality AI-driven cyberattack attribution — these disputes involve sovereignty not contracts.

The PCA deep fracture lies in the collision between sovereign immunity and AI accountability. Article 1(2) provides that state consent to arbitration constitutes waiver of jurisdictional immunity but can AI system conduct be attributed to states? If an autonomous weapons system makes an attack decision is it state action or system action? SVAMC Guideline 6 non-delegation principle carries more profound implications in international law — delegatus non potest delegare is originally an administrative law principle but when applied to states delegating military decisions to AI it touches the definition of sovereignty itself. The PCA is the only possible institutional home for these disputes yet its rules are wholly unprepared for Agent participation in interstate disputes. This is not a rule amendment problem — it requires updating the foundations of international law.

## Chapter 9: CRCICA Cairo Regional Centre for International Commercial Arbitration
## 第九章：开罗区域国际商事仲裁中心

### Institutional Profile

## About the Cairo Regional Centre for International Commercial Arbitration (CRCICA)
The Cairo Regional Centre for International Commercial Arbitration (CRCICA) was established in 1979 under the auspices of the Asian-African Legal Consultative Committee (AALCC), making it one of the oldest arbitral institutions in the developing world. Located in Cairo, Egypt, CRCICA serves as the premier regional center for international commercial arbitration in Africa and the Middle East.
CRCICA was established in response to the need for fair, inexpensive, and expeditious dispute resolution procedures for economic and commercial transactions involving parties from developing countries. The institution operates as an independent, non-profit organization governed by its own statutes and regulations.
CRCICA provides comprehensive arbitration services, including institutional administration of arbitrations conducted under the UNCITRAL Arbitration Rules (as modified for CRCICA), facilities and support for ad hoc arbitrations, assistance with enforcement of arbitral awards, and training and education in international arbitration.
The CRCICA Arbitration Rules (2022) incorporate the UNCITRAL Arbitration Rules as the basis for proceedings, with certain modifications and adaptations tailored to the needs of parties using the Centre. The Rules are available in English, French, and Arabic, reflecting the multilingual character of the region.
**【中文翻译】**
开罗区域国际商业仲裁中心（CRCICA）于1979年在亚洲-非洲法律协商委员会（AALCC）的主持下成立，是发展中世界最古老的仲裁机构之一。CRCICA总部位于埃及开罗，是非洲和中东地区首屈一指的区域国际商业仲裁中心。
CRCICA的成立是为了满足涉及发展中国家当事人经济和商业交易的公平、经济和快速争议解决程序的需要。该机构作为独立非营利组织运作，由其自身的章程和条例管理。
CRCICA提供全面的仲裁服务，包括依据《联合国国际贸易法委员会仲裁规则》（经CRCICA修订版）进行的机构管理仲裁，为临时仲裁提供设施和支持，协助执行仲裁裁决，以及国际仲裁培训和教育。
2022年《CRCICA仲裁规则》以《联合国国际贸易法委员会仲裁规则》为程序基础，并作了针对使用该中心当事人的需要的某些修改和适应性调整。该规则有英文、法文和阿拉伯文版本，反映了该地区多语言的特点。

### Arbitration Rules (English Original)

## CRCICA ARBITRATION RULES 2022

### ARTICLE 1 – DEFINITIONS

**1.1** In these Rules:
(a) "Centre" means the Cairo Regional Centre for International Commercial Arbitration;
(b) "Director" means the Director of the Centre and includes any officer acting on his behalf;
(c) "Party" means a party to the arbitration and includes any person who has been joined as an additional party to the arbitration;
(d) "Arbitral Tribunal" means a sole arbitrator or a panel of arbitrators, as the case may be;
(e) "Claimant" means the party initiating the arbitration proceedings;
(f) "Respondent" means the other party to the arbitration;
(g) "Request for Arbitration" means the request for arbitration filed by the Claimant with the Centre.

### ARTICLE 2 – SCOPE OF APPLICATION

**2.1** These Rules apply to arbitration under the auspices of the Centre.

**2.2** The Centre shall administer arbitrations in accordance with these Rules.

**2.3** These Rules shall apply subject to any agreement between the parties.

### ARTICLE 3 – NOTICE OF ARBITRATION

**3.1** The Claimant shall send a Notice of Arbitration in writing to the Respondent and to the Centre.

**3.2** The date of receipt of the Notice of Arbitration by the Respondent shall be deemed to be the date of commencement of the arbitration proceedings.

**3.3** The Notice of Arbitration shall contain:
(a) The names and addresses of the parties;
(b) Reference to the contract or other instrument out of or in relation to which the dispute arises;
(c) Reference to the arbitration agreement;
(d) A statement of the nature of the dispute and an indication of the amount involved, if quantifiable;
(e) A brief statement of the claim;
(f) The Relief or remedy sought;
(g) The Claimant's proposal as to the number of arbitrators, their qualifications and the seat and language of the arbitration, if not already agreed;
(h) The Claimant's nomination of an arbitrator, if the parties have agreed that the Arbitral Tribunal shall be composed in this manner.

### ARTICLE 4 – RESPONSE TO THE NOTICE OF ARBITRATION

**4.1** Within 30 days from receipt of the Notice of Arbitration, the Respondent shall send a Response to the Notice of Arbitration in writing to the Claimant and to the Centre.

**4.2** The Response shall contain:
(a) The name and address of the Respondent;
(b) Confirmation or denial of all or part of the claims;
(c) A brief statement of the facts and law relevant to the dispute;
(d) Any counterclaim, together with the amount involved, if quantifiable;
(e) The Respondent's proposal as to the number of arbitrators, their qualifications and the seat and language of the arbitration, if not already agreed;
(f) The Respondent's nomination of an arbitrator, if the parties have agreed that the Arbitral Tribunal shall be composed in this manner.

### ARTICLE 5 – NUMBER OF ARBITRATORS

**5.1** The number of arbitrators shall be determined in accordance with the agreement of the parties.

**5.2** If the parties have not agreed on the number of arbitrators, the Centre shall determine the number, taking into account the complexity and amount in dispute.

### ARTICLE 6 – CONSTITUTION OF THE ARBITRAL TRIBUNAL

**6.1** In an arbitration with a sole arbitrator, the parties shall jointly nominate the arbitrator within 30 days after the receipt of the Response to the Notice of Arbitration by the Claimant.

**6.2** If the parties fail to nominate a sole arbitrator within the time limit, the Centre shall appoint the arbitrator.

**6.3** In an arbitration with three arbitrators:
(a) Each party shall nominate one arbitrator;
(b) The two arbitrators so nominated shall appoint the third arbitrator, who shall be the presiding arbitrator;
(c) If a party fails to nominate an arbitrator within the time limit, the Centre shall appoint the arbitrator.

**6.4** If the two party-nominated arbitrators fail to appoint the third arbitrator within 30 days of the nomination of the second arbitrator, the Centre shall appoint the third arbitrator.

**6.5** If there are multiple Claimants or multiple Respondents, the multiple Claimants or multiple Respondents shall jointly nominate the arbitrator in accordance with this Article.

### ARTICLE 7 – QUALIFICATIONS OF ARBITRATORS

**7.1** Every arbitrator must be and remain impartial and independent of the parties throughout the arbitral proceedings.

**7.2** Every arbitrator shall, upon appointment, disclose in writing to the parties and the Centre any circumstances likely to give rise to justifiable doubts as to his or her impartiality or independence.

**7.3** An arbitrator shall disclose to the parties and the Centre any such circumstances that arise during the arbitral proceedings.

### ARTICLE 8 – CHALLENGE OF ARBITRATORS

**8.1** A party may challenge an arbitrator if circumstances exist that give rise to justifiable doubts as to the arbitrator's impartiality or independence.

**8.2** A party intending to challenge an arbitrator shall send notice of the challenge to the Centre and to the other party within 15 days after becoming aware of the circumstances giving rise to the challenge.

**8.3** The notice of challenge shall be in writing and shall state the reasons for the challenge.

**8.4** The Centre shall decide on the challenge after consulting with the arbitrator concerned and the other party.

**8.5** The decision of the Centre shall be final.

### ARTICLE 9 – REPLACEMENT OF ARBITRATORS

**9.1** An arbitrator's mandate shall terminate if:
(a) The Centre accepts the challenge;
(b) The arbitrator resigns;
(c) The arbitrator dies;
(d) The Centre decides that the arbitrator is not fulfilling his or her duties.

**9.2** If an arbitrator is replaced, the proceedings shall resume at the stage they had reached when the vacancy occurred, unless the Tribunal decides otherwise.

### ARTICLE 10 – CONDUCT OF PROCEEDINGS

**10.1** The Arbitral Tribunal shall conduct the proceedings in an efficient and cost-effective manner.

**10.2** The Arbitral Tribunal shall treat the parties with equality and shall give each party a reasonable opportunity to present its case.

**10.3** The Arbitral Tribunal shall determine the procedure to be followed, taking into account the views of the parties.

**10.4** The Arbitral Tribunal may hold hearings for the presentation of evidence and for oral argument.

### ARTICLE 11 – STATEMENT OF CLAIM AND DEFENCE

**11.1** Within a period of time to be fixed by the Arbitral Tribunal, the Claimant shall submit a Statement of Claim containing:
(a) A detailed statement of facts;
(b) A statement of the legal grounds or arguments supporting each claim;
(c) The Relief or remedy sought.

**11.2** Within a period of time to be fixed by the Arbitral Tribunal, the Respondent shall submit a Statement of Defence containing:
(a) A detailed statement of defence to the Statement of Claim;
(b) Any counterclaim, together with the legal grounds or arguments supporting it;
(c) The Relief or remedy sought in relation to any counterclaim.

**11.3** Each party shall annex to its statement all documents on which it relies.

### ARTICLE 12 – EVIDENCE

**12.1** Each party shall prove the facts on which it relies for its claims or defence.

**12.2** The Arbitral Tribunal may order the parties to produce documents, exhibits or other evidence within such time as it may determine.

**12.3** The Arbitral Tribunal may appoint experts to report on specific issues.

**12.4** The Arbitral Tribunal shall determine the admissibility, relevance, materiality and weight of evidence.

### ARTICLE 13 – HEARINGS

**13.1** If the Arbitral Tribunal decides to hold a hearing, it shall give the parties adequate notice of the date, time and place of the hearing.

**13.2** Hearings shall be held in private unless the parties agree otherwise.

**13.3** The Arbitral Tribunal may record the hearings in any manner it considers appropriate.

### ARTICLE 14 – DEFAULT

**14.1** If the Claimant fails to submit the Statement of Claim, the Arbitral Tribunal may order the termination of the proceedings.

**14.2** If the Respondent fails to submit the Statement of Defence, the Arbitral Tribunal shall continue the proceedings.

**14.3** If a party fails to appear at a hearing or to produce documentary evidence, the Arbitral Tribunal may continue the proceedings and make the award on the evidence before it.

### ARTICLE 15 – CLOSURE OF PROCEEDINGS

**15.1** When satisfied that the parties have had a reasonable opportunity to present their cases, the Arbitral Tribunal shall declare the proceedings closed.

### ARTICLE 16 – MAKING OF AWARD AND TIME LIMITS

**16.1** The Arbitral Tribunal shall make the award within a reasonable time after the closure of proceedings.

**16.2** The Centre may extend the time for making the award upon the request of the Arbitral Tribunal or on its own initiative.

### ARTICLE 17 – AWARD

**17.1** The Arbitral Tribunal shall make the award in writing.

**17.2** The award shall state the reasons on which it is based, unless the parties have agreed that no reasons are to be given.

**17.3** The award shall state the date and place of the arbitration.

**17.4** The award shall be signed by the arbitrators. If there is more than one arbitrator and any arbitrator refuses to sign, the signature of the majority shall be sufficient, provided that the reason for the omission of the signature is stated.

**17.5** The award shall be sent to the parties and to the Centre.

**17.6** The award shall be final and binding on the parties from the date it is made.

**17.7** By agreeing to arbitration under these Rules, the parties undertake to carry out any award without delay.

### ARTICLE 18 – INTERPRETATION AND CORRECTION OF AWARD

**18.1** Within 30 days after receipt of the award, any party may, with notice to the other party, request:
(a) Interpretation of the award;
(b) Correction of any clerical, typographical or similar errors in the award.

**18.2** The Arbitral Tribunal shall make the interpretation or correction in writing within 30 days after receipt of the request.

**18.3** The interpretation or correction shall form part of the award.

### ARTICLE 19 – ADDITIONAL AWARD

**19.1** Within 30 days after receipt of the award, any party may, with notice to the other party, request that the Arbitral Tribunal make an additional award as to claims presented in the proceedings but not decided in the award.

**19.2** The Arbitral Tribunal shall make the additional award within 60 days after receipt of the request.

### ARTICLE 20 – SETTLEMENT

**20.1** If the parties reach a settlement during the proceedings, the Arbitral Tribunal shall terminate the proceedings if so requested by the parties.

**20.2** If the parties request, the Arbitral Tribunal may record the settlement in the form of a consent award.

### ARTICLE 21 – COSTS

**21.1** The costs of arbitration shall be fixed by the Centre in accordance with its Schedule of Costs in force at the time of the commencement of the arbitration.

**21.2** The costs of arbitration shall in principle be borne by the unsuccessful party.

**21.3** The Arbitral Tribunal may apportion the costs between the parties if it determines that such apportionment is reasonable.

### ARTICLE 22 – DEPOSIT OF COSTS

**22.1** The Centre may require the parties to deposit amounts in advance to cover the costs of arbitration.

**22.2** If the required deposit is not made, the Centre may suspend or terminate the arbitration.

### ARTICLE 23 – WAIVER

**23.1** A party who knows that any provision of these Rules or any agreed procedural requirement has not been complied with, and who fails to state promptly its objection to such non-compliance, shall be deemed to have waived its right to object.

### ARTICLE 24 – CONFIDENTIALITY

**24.1** The Centre, the Arbitral Tribunal and the parties shall maintain the confidentiality of the arbitration.

**24.2** The Centre may publish information concerning the arbitration, provided that no party is identified by name.

### ARTICLE 25 – EXCLUSION OF LIABILITY

**25.1** Neither the Centre nor any arbitrator shall be liable to any person for any act or omission in connection with any arbitration under these Rules, unless such limitation of liability is excluded by applicable law.

### ARTICLE 26 – SEAT AND LANGUAGE

**26.1** The seat of arbitration shall be determined in accordance with the agreement of the parties. In the absence of such agreement, the Centre shall determine the seat.

**26.2** The language of arbitration shall be determined in accordance with the agreement of the parties. In the absence of such agreement, the Arbitral Tribunal shall determine the language.

### ARTICLE 27 – APPLICABLE LAW

**27.1** The Arbitral Tribunal shall decide the dispute in accordance with the rules of law chosen by the parties or agreed upon by them.

**27.2** Failing such choice or agreement, the Arbitral Tribunal shall apply the rules of law it determines to be appropriate.

**27.3** The Arbitral Tribunal shall decide as amiables compositeurs only if the parties have expressly authorized it to do so.

### ARTICLE 28 – INTERIM MEASURES

**28.1** At the request of a party, the Arbitral Tribunal may grant interim measures in accordance with the applicable law.

**28.2** The Arbitral Tribunal may order the provision of appropriate security in connection with any such measures.

### ARTICLE 29 – GENERAL PROVISIONS

**29.1** In all matters not expressly provided for in these Rules, the Centre, the Arbitral Tribunal and the parties shall act in the spirit of these Rules and shall make every effort to ensure that the award is enforceable at law.

**Fresa Li:**

CRCICA Arbitration Rules play an increasingly important role in dispute resolution across Africa and the Middle East. The 2022 edition brings the CRCICA framework into closer alignment with international mainstream standards while preserving local characteristics that serve African and Middle Eastern parties. CRCICA has become the most representative arbitral institution in the region. As a practitioner, I note that CRCICA's Article 7 on the arbitral tribunal's authority to rule on its own jurisdiction borrows effectively from the UNCITRAL competence-competence model, while its cost schedule is calibrated to the local economic environment—a thoughtful balance between international credibility and accessibility. For disputes involving energy, infrastructure, or construction projects in North Africa.

---


**Dr. Venessa:**

CRCICA 2022 Rules are based on UNCITRAL with trilingual versions (English French Arabic) reflecting the developing world multilingual reality. Article 7 emphasizes arbitrator impartiality and independence inheriting the UNCITRAL model. As the oldest arbitral institution in Africa and the Middle East CRCICA serves the region with the most uneven technological infrastructure.

This unevenness is CRCICA greatest institutional challenge in the Agent era. While SIAC and HKIAC advance digitization and AI governance CRCICA parties may lack reliable internet access. Agent arbitration presupposes digital infrastructure and the digital divide means Agent arbitration may widen rather than bridge the arbitration justice gap. SVAMC AI Guideline 1 assumes all participants can understand each AI tools relevant limitations biases and risks — an assumption that may not hold in CRCICA service region. If Agent arbitration becomes mainstream CRCICA parties may face structural procedural disadvantage due to lack of Agent capabilities — a disadvantage harder to bridge than economic inequality. CRCICA imperative is not how to introduce Agent arbitration but how to safeguard its parties procedural equality in the Agent era.
